# READING TEST

In the Reading test, you will read a variety of texts and answer several different types of reading comprehension questions. The entire Reading test will last 75 minutes. There are three parts, and directions are given for each part. You are encouraged to answer as many questions as possible within the time allowed.

You must mark your answers on the separate answer sheet. Do not write your answers in your test book.

### Questions 147-148 refer to the following notice.

**Closure on September 30–October 4**

This notice concerns Galloway Corporation employees at our Cassidy location who commute by car. The parking area at this branch will be unavailable from Wednesday, September 30 through Sunday, October 4 while work crews resurface and repaint it. Affected employees are encouraged to park in City Garage 5 on Hirsch Street and use our employee portal to submit their receipts for reimbursement. Alternatively, they may work from home with the permission of their manager.

### Questions 153-154 refer to the following online chat discussion.

**Help Chat**

**Katherine Edwards [3:32 P.M.]**  
Thank you for contacting Customer Support for BrinkMaps. How can I help you?

**Brandon Oakley [3:33 P.M.]**  
I own a small exercise class business, and I want it to appear on your maps. But when I tried to register on your site, I got a message saying, "Evidence not sufficient." I'm not sure what the problem is. My case number is 0935584.

**Katherine Edwards [3:35 P.M.]**

**Brandon Oakley [3:36 P.M.]**

### Questions 155-157 refer to the following e-mail.

- **From:** <a.yoshida@sommerauto.com>
- **To:** <d.mueller@sommerauto.com>
- **Subject:** Request
- **Date:** August 3

Dear Mr. Mueller,

You may be aware that investor confidence in Sommer Automotive has been shaken by the low sales of this year's new vehicle models. To show that we are taking this issue seriously, we have decided to invite investors, analysts, and media representatives to our headquarters for an Investor Day in September. The CEO and executive vice president for product development will give speeches on our business strategy and upcoming products, as well as take questions from attendees. The entire event will be broadcast over the Internet for the benefit of investors who are unable to attend and any interested members of the public.

We are also considering adding a session in which we let investors take rides in the self-driving cars your division is developing. Do you think their performance is reliable enough that the experience would be an impressive one? Please let me know as soon as possible so that we can finalize the schedule for the event. Thank you.

Sincerely,

Akari Yoshida  
Public Relations Director  
Sommer Automotive

Hello, everyone,

Elena

### Questions 161-164 refer to the following e-mail.

- **From:** Roxanne Malloy <roxiemalloy@mci-mail.com>
- **To:** <customerservice@naviairlines.com>
- **Subject:** Dissatisfied customer
- **Date:** November 17

To Whom It May Concern,

I have been flying with Navi Airlines for over 15 years and became a member of your Mileage Club four years ago. I have had many positive experiences with your company, particularly during international travel.

However, I have noticed an unfortunate trend in your domestic flights, which I take the most often. On my most recent flight last week, the flight attendant apparently forgot that I ordered a snack, and I had to remind her twice and wait over 10 minutes before she finally brought it to my seat. She did not apologize for the mistake and in fact acted annoyed with me for the rest of the flight.

I have had similar experiences on my other recent domestic flights. Last month, a flight attendant rudely refused to help stow my carry-on bag during boarding. And when I visited the Navi Airlines service counter at Hoyle Airport after last week's flight to make a complaint, no one was there.

I frequently travel between Hoyle and Lofton for business, and since Navi Airlines operates the most non-stop flights for that route, I would really like to continue flying with you. If this deterioration in service quality continues, however, I may have to take my business to another carrier.

Sincerely,

Roxanne Malloy

161. What aspect of Navi Airlines is Ms. Malloy dissatisfied with?
     (A) The frequent flight delays
     (B) The poor customer service
     (C) The careless baggage handling
     (D) The badly designed plane seating

     (C) comfort

     (D) It was built 15 years ago.

### Questions 168-171 refer to the following contract.

**Contract between**  
**Malone Group, Inc.**  
**and**  
**Duncan University**

I. This contract between Malone Group and Duncan University concerns the technology claimed in U.S. Patent No. 11994503, _Solar Shingles and Base with Wireless Power Transfer_, which was developed by Duncan University researcher Chae-Won Ham. — [1] —. A copy of the patent is provided in Appendix A.

II. Duncan University hereby grants Malone Group a non-exclusive license to use the aforementioned technology in products to be developed and sold within the United States. — [2] —. Duncan University retains the right to make use of the technology for research and educational purposes. — [3] —. In return for the license, Malone Group shall pay Duncan University a one-time licensing fee of $50,000 plus 8% of net sales from the products.

III. Malone Group agrees to make efforts to follow the projected development timeline outlined in Appendix B and to furnish Duncan University with a written update on these efforts at the end of each quarter. Failure to reach the agreed-upon milestones may be grounds for Duncan University to terminate this contract. — [4] —. The terms of the development timeline may be revised with both parties' consent.

### Questions 172-175 refer to the following text-message chain.

**Emmett Woods [11:02 A.M.]**  
Hilda, I finished researching the recent selling prices of properties like Mr. Hayashi's house, and my findings are very promising. I think we'll be able to ask for quite a high amount for it.

**Hilda Rivera [11:03 A.M.]**  
That's great news, Emmett. And it means that we'll want to make sure the listing gets a lot of attention. Instead of taking the photos ourselves, let's hire Burton Media to take professional ones.

**Rebecca Knight [11:04 A.M.]**  
I can set that up. Do you have a preferred day and time for the photo shoot?

**Hilda Rivera [11:06 A.M.]**  
Any opening on my calendar is fine with me, but sometime between this Thursday and Sunday would probably be most convenient for Mr. Hayashi and his family. They'll be away on vacation then.

**Emmett Woods [11:07 A.M.]**  
Hmm, when we've used Burton Media in the past, we've always had to wait at least a week for an appointment. If you're set on that timeframe, Hilda, we'll probably need to look into other photographers. I've heard that Wagner Photography does good work.

**Rebecca Knight [11:08 A.M.]**  
OK, I'll contact them. But I'll check Burton Media's availability first, just in case.

**Hilda Rivera [11:10 A.M.]**  
I'd appreciate that. And remember to have the property information at hand when you make the calls. They'll want to know the square footage and number of rooms to get a sense of how long the appointment will need to be.

172. Where do the writers most likely work?
     (A) At a travel agency
     (B) At a real estate firm
     (C) At a photography studio
     (D) At a house-cleaning company

     (B) His family owns the writers' company.
     (C) He is going out of town soon.
     (D) He wants to be present for a photo shoot.

### Questions 176-180 refer to the following advertisement and memo.

**Horizon**  
_One of SmartBiz.com's '8 Most Useful Business Applications'!_

Does your store still use traditional cash registers? It's time to upgrade! Horizon is a software application that not only handles purchases and refunds as quickly and reliably as traditional point-of-sale systems but also provides a wealth of additional features—all for a reasonable price.

**Features**

- **Hardware:** Application users receive a discount on the Horizon Register, which takes the latest contactless payment methods.
- **Sales Tracking:** Collect data to determine your most popular items and evaluate the effectiveness of sales promotions.
- **Inventory Management:** An alert will warn you when stock of any item falls below a preset level.
- **Help Line:** Instead of searching through a manual, receive immediate assistance by phone (newly available around-the-clock).

**MEMO**

- **From:** Marshall Quinn, Greenways Manager
- **To:** All staff
- **Date:** February 23
- **Subject:** Announcement

Greenways' owner has decided to replace the cash registers on our checkout counter with Horizon point-of-sale systems, which consist of specialized registers loaded with a software application. While this is a big change that may cause some difficulties at first, please rest assured it will make your jobs easier in the long term, as well as opening up possibilities for the store's growth. I am particularly excited about some expanded capabilities that the Horizon Register will give us.

We will close on Monday and Tuesday, March 6–7, for the transition. On Monday, a Horizon technician will do the installation and help me enter all of our golf equipment and clothing into the system as merchandise. On Tuesday, I will lead two training sessions: one from 11 A.M. to noon and one from 3 to 4 P.M. Everyone needs to attend one of these so that you will all be ready to use the system during your next scheduled shift. We will then have a few weeks to become fully adept before the influx of customers we typically see in April.

I am writing to provide you with more information about the seminars you will be leading here at Merrick Library on Thursday, August 21.

First, please see the attached schedule for an overview of the day. For the lunchtime break, you are welcome to bring food to eat in our staff lounge, or I can recommend a nearby restaurant.

Second, the library has been renovated since your last visit, so when you first arrive, please stop at the circulation desk and ask for Roberta Miles, our programming assistant. She will show you to the new location of our seminar room, as well as help you get any technology set up.

Janice Fletcher

### Questions 191-195 refer to the following press release, form, and e-mail.

**FOR IMMEDIATE RELEASE**  
April 10  
Contact: Holly Costello, 555-0185

Sandel Media is now accepting nominations for the annual Sandel Marketing Awards. "Sandies," as the awards are nicknamed, recognize outstanding work across the marketing industry. They celebrate achievements in various client sectors and marketing channels, as well as naming one exceptional agency "Marketer of the Year," a title won last year by ENVZ of Shipham. This year's competition, which sees the introduction of a new Sandie, "Best Use of Influencers," promises to highlight an even more impressive array of talent.

Anyone can submit a nomination by visiting www.sandelmagazine.com/sandies/nom and filling out the provided form. The nomination period will close on Friday, May 24. Winners will be notified on July 1 and invited to a ceremony to be held on the evening of Saturday, July 20 in Chadwick.

About Sandel Media: Sandel Media publishes _Sandel Magazine_, a trusted source for marketing news and analysis. It is headquartered in Kenyon.

**Nomination Form**

- **Name:** Cheryl Stamper
- **Affiliation:** Director of Public Relations, Gladney, Inc.

- **Name of agency:** Ellsworth Partners
- **Name of campaign:** Gladney Works For You
- **Name of client:** Gladney, Inc.

The long-form videos Ellsworth Partners created were visually stunning and yet explained Gladney's products and services in an accessible way. Your judges can watch them at www.gladney.com/gwfy.

- **Subject:** FWD: Sandies
- **Date:** July 1

Hi Leonore,

I just received the attached e-mail from Sandel Media—apparently, our agency has been chosen as runner-up for the Sandie for Best Campaign in the Financial Services Sector! Unfortunately, runners-up have to pay a $1,500 fee to attend the awards ceremony and put a Sandie badge on their company materials.

I think the benefits are worth it. The badge would impress potential clients, and I've heard from Marcus, whose old agency in Halcomb won a Sandie, that the great networking opportunity. Plus, I have family in the city it's being held in, so our agency wouldn't have to pay for accommodations if I went. What do you think?

—Dean

### Questions 196-200 refer to the following e-mails and order form.

- **From:** lynnetchua@tilmail.com
- **To:** customerservice@medleysg.sg
- **Subject:** Medley Ranch Dressing
- **Date:** 2 December

Hello,

While visiting my favourite American-style diner this week, I noticed that the ranch salad dressing wasn't Medley Ranch Dressing anymore. The server explained that your company had recently decided to stop selling it in Singapore.

This was quite disappointing to hear, as I have been a fan of Medley Ranch Dressing since I studied abroad in the USA twenty years ago. I stayed with an American family that always used to have that big, round bottle on the dinner table, and now the tangy, creamy taste brings up good memories of that period for me.

So I am wondering—would it be possible to buy a few leftover bottles of the dressing, if you have them? I don't mind buying a case, because I remember that it has a long shelf life. Please let me know.

Sincerely,

Lynette Chua

---

- **From:** customerservice@medleysg.sg
- **To:** lynnetchua@tilmail.com
- **Subject:** Re: Medley Ranch Dressing
- **Date:** 4 December
- **Attachment:** Order_form

Dear Ms. Chua,

Thank you for contacting Medley Singapore. I'm pleased to inform you that we do have a small quantity of Medley Ranch Dressing left. I have attached a form you can use to order some.

We were sorry to discontinue the product in Singapore. We had hoped that the Medley Ranch Dressing in its slim, sleek bottle would appeal to Singaporeans' cosmopolitan tastes. However, there just isn't much demand for creamy salad dressings here, and we were unsuccessful in convincing people to use the product in marinades, dips, and sandwiches like our American customers do. Our management had to make the smart business decision.

We do still offer one creamy dressing, and I'd be happy to send you a sample of it as a token of our appreciation for your loyalty to the Medley brand. If you're interested, please reply with your mailing address.

Sincerely,

Justin Tan  
Customer Service Representative  
Medley Singapore

---

Management has decided to upgrade the security of your residences by installing Veratite smart locks. In light of the Environmental Sustainability Agency's recent announcement of tighter restrictions on the discharge of wastewater, I wanted to remind you that we are available to assist Farley Meat Company.

You should also be aware of a resource we have created entitled "Upcoming Changes to ESA Wastewater Regulations." ### Port of Hutchley Getting Back on Track

**By Michael Solis**

A software issue forced the Port of Hutchley to shut down yesterday. Port officials have not explained the nature of the issue, only emphasizing that it was not the result of a cyberattack. [1] —.

The closure caused congestion both on land and at sea. Aubrey Knight, who was transporting a shipment of appliances from Travisville, was one of many left waiting on Hutchley's roadways for the port to reopen. [2] —. "There was a line of trucks stretching as far back as I could see," Ms. Knight said about Highway 32. Similarly, at least four container ships were unable to dock and had to anchor off the coast. [3] —.

Fortunately, the shutdown did not create a backlog at South Terminal because its gate is closed on Sundays. The port's other three terminals were ultimately able to reopen, but not until nearly 5 P.M., their usual closing time. [4] —. Port authorities announced they would maintain extended hours through the end of the week.

Hendrick's
322 Conner Street

Dear Manager,

I would like to offer your store the opportunity to support the young athletes of Burnet by becoming a sponsor of the Burnet Youth Baseball League (BYBL).

The BYBL is a volunteer-run nonprofit organization that serves children aged 9–14. Last season, more than 200 children played for our 14 teams in three age divisions. We are proud to be instilling important values such as teamwork and sportsmanship in young generations.

The BYBL depends on sponsorships to fully cover our expenses, which include uniforms and baseball gear, field maintenance, and wages for umpires.

Sergio Padilla

Questions 176-180 refer to the following article and job posting.

### Dance for the Gold to Move Filming Location

SYDNEY (20 February)—The Australian Broadcasting System (AAB) announced today that its reality competition show _Dance for the Gold_ will no longer be filmed at Sydney's Auburn Studios, which has been booked by several major film projects. Instead, its sixth season will be shot at Hobsons Studios in Melbourne, far from the network's Sydney headquarters. "We looked at facilities in Brisbane and Perth as well, but only Hobsons has a soundstage with the capacity to comfortably hold our live audience," explained Lauren Campbell, ABS's head of production.

Anticipation is high for _Dance for the Gold_'s sixth season after its highly rated fifth season, even though popular judge Santino Nucci will not be returning. Based on an idea attributed to Ms. Campbell, the program features aspiring professional dancers who compete to master a variety of dance styles for the approval of expert judges. The new episodes will begin airing in June.

---

### Australian Broadcasting System (ABS)

**Job Title: Costume Designer**

ABS is seeking a creative, reliable professional to join the on-site production crew for the sixth season of _Dance for the Gold_.

**Responsibilities:**

- Create outfits for multiple contestants within a strict weekly timeframe
- Collaborate effectively with other members of the costume department, as well as contestants and various production departments

**Qualifications:**

- Membership in the Australian Costume Design Guild
- Experience with designing costumes for productions involving dance
- Ability to use computer-aided design software

Interested applicants should send a résumé and digital portfolio to oliver.gaines@abs.co.au by 5 March.

Questions 181-185 refer to the following online review and response.

www.hoot-reviews.com/goal-fitness-wolfsburg

### Goal Fitness

**Reviewer:** Holly Yoshida, Wolfsburg  
**Date Posted:** November 30  
⭐⭐☆☆☆

My first few weeks at Goal Fitness have been a mixed experience. There was no one at the front desk the day that I came by to sign up; I finally found an employee over by the treadmills. She appeared to be the only person working that day, which seemed like a bad sign. Still, this place is really close to my work, so I decided to give it a try anyway. Its good points are that the exercise equipment is in good condition, and there's never a long wait to use any of it because the gym isn't crowded. The one fitness class I've taken, though, was intense. I understand that some people like to be pushed to their limits, but I work out to feel good. I didn't like how the instructor kept yelling at us to try harder.

---

Dear Ms. Yoshida,

Thank you for your review. I am sorry to hear that we made a poor first impression. It was partly the result of unfortunate timing, as I happened to be on vacation that week. I also want to reassure you that we have hired several new employees since that time.

Group classes are an important benefit of Goal Fitness membership, so I want to help you find a satisfactory one. Based on your review, I would recommend Cardio Dance. That said, I would be happy to talk to you about other options, including personal training. Just stop by my office, which is on the far side of the counter where our nutritional supplements and workout gear are sold. If you do come by, I can offer you a complimentary Quench Flask with our logo on it. It is great for keeping you hydrated during workouts.

Nathan Browning, Goal Fitness Manager

### Tech Helpers

Galindo Public Library's collection now includes a wealth of digital content, from e-books to streaming films. We are recruiting volunteer "Tech Helpers" to assist patrons with accessing our electronic resources, in addition to teaching basic tech skills such as sending e-mail.

**Requirements:**

- Considerable proficiency with computers, mobile devices, and the Internet
- Positive demeanor and the ability to explain concepts clearly and patiently
- Minimum commitment of two hours per week for six months

To express interest in becoming a Tech Helper, send an e-mail to volunteer@galindopubliclibrary.org.

Questions 155-157 refer to the following article.

## City Approves Transformation of Pinebrook Mall Site

Oct. 11—The city of Hawthorn has approved a proposal to redevelop Pinebrook Mall, which has sat empty for the past year. After demolishing most of the structure and its parking area, developer Austin Partners plans to build a large apartment complex bordered by an office building, a few new storefronts, and a community park with a playground and sports fields.

Austin Partners conceived the project after the recent relaxation of Hawthorn's zoning laws to allow some mixed-use developments in commercial zones—a change made to help the city meet increasing demand for housing. The influx of people hired by Hawthorn's booming medical technology firms has put a strain on its existing supply.

"Hawthorn doesn't have much completely undeveloped land anymore, but it does have a few underused retail complexes," said Geeta Nathwani, Austin Partners' vice president of development. "It's a reflection of the rise of online shopping. We hope that the successful redevelopment of Pinebrook Mall will lead to similar projects by demonstrating the potential of this approach."

Questions 168-171 refer to the following article.

PHILADELPHIA (April 7)—Fast food chain Zora's announced yesterday that it will begin rolling out an artificial intelligence (AI) chatbot to take orders for its drive-through service in June. — [1] —.

Company officials explained that drive-through customers who pull up to one of the chain's new ordering stations will be able to speak their order out loud or ask questions about the menu just as they could to a human worker. — [2] —. The technology will handle the interaction and transmit the order to the restaurant's staff for preparation.

"This project came out of a desire to reduce waiting times for our drive-through customers by speeding up the ordering process," said Zora's CEO Curtis Hendricks.

The chatbot will be powered by software developed by Innovix that can recognize the meaning of speech and generate appropriate responses in natural language. Zora's software engineers have been working with Innovix to customize the chatbot for its task. — [3] —.

"The team has put a lot of effort into avoiding potential pitfalls," said Mr. Hendricks. "For example, they've taught the chatbot all of the substitute terms that people like to use when ordering." He cited customers' habits of calling the chain's Big Bacon Cheeseburger a "BBC" and using the generic term "milkshake" for its Chiller desserts.

— [4] —. Despite such reassurances, the news has received a largely negative response from the public. Many social media users expressed suspicion that the move was actually a way for Zora's, the fifth-largest restaurant chain in the U.S., to save money on personnel costs.

# The Preston Blaze 7005 Heater

Keep working throughout the winter with the Preston Blaze 7005 space heater, the perfect heating solution for industrial sites of 40–60 square metres in size. Its high and low heat settings and thermostatic control make it easy to reach and maintain a comfortable temperature. The Blaze 7005 is simple to run, as it utilizes electric power—no special fuel necessary! It also emits no exhaust fumes that must be ventilated, like oil- or gas-powered heaters do.

At just 7.5 kilograms, the Blaze 7005 is easily portable. Rubber insulation on the handle makes it possible to reposition the heater even when it is operating at full strength. It is competitively priced at £

110, inclusive of Preston's standard 12-month warranty. Up to three years of additional coverage may be purchased for a reasonable fee.

---

⭐ ⭐ ⭐ ⭐ ⯪ (4.5/5 stars)

I bought two Preston Blaze 7005s last month for the business I manage. We use them every day, and I love how they warm up the space. We make custom cabinets and bookshelves, and you can't do that work with cold, stiff hands. That hasn't been a problem since we put a Blaze 7005 on each end of the workshop.

The only things I don't like about this product are that the fan is noisy and the power cable has an industrial plug. This means you can only connect it with special electrical outlets. I understand that this is required for safety, but it still limits where the Blaze 7005 can be used.

Rory Miles, 22 January

# Seeking Musical Acts!

The Dornsby Growers Alliance will begin including live musical performances at our weekly farmers market to create a more festive atmosphere for shoppers. Visit our Web site to apply!

A stage and basic sound equipment will be provided. The budget for compensation is limited, but tip collection will be allowed. Performers must play two hours of family-friendly music suitable for all ages at a volume compliant with Dornsby City's noise ordinances. Out-of-town acts are welcome.

**About the Market:**  
The Dornsby Farmers Market is held from 9 A.M. to 1 P.M. every Saturday from May through October. The location is the section of Fourth Street between Stone Avenue and Page Street, or in case of bad weather, Bowen Hall at the Dornsby Fairgrounds, near the intersection of Warner Lane and Quinn Road.

Directions: In this part you will read a selection of texts, such as magazine and newspaper articles, e-mails, and instant messages. Each text or set of texts is followed by several questions. Select the best answer for each question and mark the letter (A), (B), (C), or (D) on your answer sheet.

Questions 147-148 refer to the following review.

**Customer Review: Merchandise Bags**  
Review posted by: Silvia Gordon

I run a small health food store and have usually stocked transparent Gaines bags to give to customers who need to carry merchandise home. These bags are made of recycled plastic but can't be recycled themselves. So, I was excited to hear about Lozano through a retail newsletter. I bought a pack of their water-soluble clear bags to try out. They truly do dissolve completely in water in just five minutes, less likely they'll end up adding to pollution in a landfill. Unfortunately, I found they also have a strange odor and slightly oily feeling, and customer experience is my top priority. I won't be making the switch.

Questions 164-167 refer to the following information.

# Invitation to Bid

**Purpose**  
The City of Rowland is seeking bids for the provision of solar-powered waste compacting bins and accompanying software. Rowland intends to modernize the sanitation system in its downtown area.

**Background**  
The metal trash and recycling bins that the city currently offers for public use downtown often overflow, and municipal workers must check their status in person. In addition to compressing waste so that more can be added, solar-powered waste compacting bins are smart devices that monitor and report their fullness levels via built-in Wi-Fi. Cities across the country have increased the efficiency of their sanitation departments by adopting them. Studies also show that the installation of solar-powered waste compacting bins has reduced the amount of litter on these municipalities' sidewalks and parkland.

**Project Specifications**  
The selected firm will supply Rowland with 70 solar-powered waste compacting bins for trash and recycling collection plus a software program to oversee them. Each bin must be able to hold at least 130 gallons of waste and include a foot pedal for hands-free opening. The bins must have a backup battery and a digital display that will clearly communicate fullness level to passers-by. The bins must be leak-proof and inaccessible to most pests.

Please submit a bid with detailed information about costs and timelines by 6 P.M. on September 4 to the City of Rowland Procurement Division.

     (A) To attract a contractor for a city project  
     (B) To describe a neighborhood's waste management issues  
     (C) To seek city workers' opinions on a proposal  
     (D) To publicize a recycling initiative

     (D) They would be emptied by a private company hired by the city.

# Data Scientist

## Ramsell Medical

### Chicago, IL

Ramsell Medical is a medical technology company headquartered in New Jersey. Together with our subsidiaries, we make equipment and software that are used in hospitals across the country. We are seeking an enthusiastic professional to join the data science team at our Chicago office. — [1] —. This opening is an opportunity to enjoy excellent work-life balance while making an impact. The data science team's insights are used to improve outcomes for thousands of patients. — [2] —.

The successful candidate will mainly be responsible for analyzing data to identify ways to improve our products and processes. — [3] —. Additional tasks will include resolving issues with data sets and creating compelling visualizations of the team's findings.

**From:** Jonah Hayes <jhayes@sproutcorner.com>  
**To:** Claire Knapp <cknapp@sproutcorner.com>  
**Subject:** Watchling videos  
**Date:** Monday, January 15  
**Attachment:** Article

Hi Claire,

I just happened to come across an article on copywriting for Watchling videos that the online magazine _Net Insights_ published on January 11. It's centered around advice for the platform's maker, but it gave me an idea for improving our plant nursery's videos, as well.

Could you read it and revise the template for our video descriptions to reflect the information Ms. Chang shares about them? Please do it before uploading our next video at the end of the week. Then I'd like you to edit our previous video descriptions as well; let's set the deadline for that task as January 26. Thanks!

—Jonah

# Improving the Copywriting for Watchling Videos

## By Hyun-Mi Chang

The video sharing platform Watchling is a great promotional tool for individuals and businesses. However, as both a video consumer and a marketing professional, I sometimes find myself frustrated with the copy that Watchling's creators write for their video titles and descriptions. Too many titles are confusingly worded or misleading about the video's content. Many long descriptions place important links to the creator's Web site or video channel at the end, where they are not visible to viewers who don't click "Show More."

Some creators may have the ability to improve their copywriting on their own. However, Watchling should also take some steps to assist video creators, who are the backbone of its business model. Free workshops on copywriting, as well as other aspects of the video production process, would be wise. The platform's technical team should consider how adjusting its algorithm could resolve some of these issues, too.

Questions 186-190 refer to the following blog post, review, and e-mail.

**http://www.coffee-guy.co.za**

# THE COFFEE GUY

## CT Coffeeworks (Cape Town)

A visit to CT Coffeeworks is a must for coffee-loving Cape Town locals and tourists. Owned by industry legend Yesmina Davids, this 42-seat café and roastery in the Central Business District has a cozy atmosphere, with plush furniture and vintage-themed décor. The friendly counter staff is knowledgeable about the menu, which spans typical items like café mochas and café lattes as well as unique offerings like a “sunrise espresso” made with orange juice. I’ve enjoyed everything I’ve tried, though in my opinion, nothing beats a simple espresso made from the café’s signature CT Blend. I recommend pairing it with their buttery, flaky croissant.

Dog owners will be happy to know that the café welcomes their furry friends on weekly Dog-Friendly Sundays.

—Charles, 14 August

**https://www.everydayreviews.co.za/ctcoffeeworks**

### CT Coffeeworks: 3/5 stars

I’m an avid reader of The Coffee Guy, Charles Maseko’s blog, and so I decided to visit CT Coffeeworks after reading his recent post about it. I arrived with a good book just after opening time yesterday, but ultimately found it difficult to concentrate on reading after some patrons brought in their energetic dogs. As for the food, I ordered an espresso and an apple tart, which looked very appetising in the display case. The espresso was rich and full-bodied with hints of chocolate, but the apple tart turned out to be bland. When I returned my dishes to the counter, the pastry chef did notice that I hadn't finished it and apologised after I explained why. All in all, it was a mixed experience and I’m not sure I’ll go there again.

Posted on 20 August by Trent Morrow (trentmorrow@go-mail.co.za)

Questions 191-195 refer to the following press release and e-mails.

# FOR IMMEDIATE RELEASE

WASHINGTON (October 12)—Julia Zhang, assistant director of the Booker Foundation for Sustainable Irrigation (BFSI), will be leaving her position after twelve years with the organization. Ms. Zhang has accepted an appointment to the National Resources Conservation Agency, where she will administer public grants for projects that promote innovation in conservation.

The BFSI is the country's largest nonprofit organization dedicated to promoting efficient irrigation practices in agriculture. In addition to providing direct support to small-scale farms to improve their watering systems, its activities include research, education, and advocacy. It is now seeking an experienced professional to replace Ms. Zhang. The ideal candidate will have a background in the field, strong problem-solving and communication skills, and enthusiasm for implementing the executive director's vision. Those interested in the position should visit bfsi.org to learn more.

---

**To:** Celine Pittman  
**From:** Khalil Malouf  
**Date:** October 20  
**Subject:** Candidate for assistant director opening

Hi Celine,

I would like to add Zane Reese, a board member at Pollard Industries, to our candidate pool for the assistant directorship. I ran into him at the Tennessee Valley Growers Trade Show last week and learned that he's interested in joining the nonprofit sector. He retired from his executive position at Pollard about a year ago but misses full-time work, and he has all the qualifications we're looking for. Could you invite him for an interview? We'll just need to clarify the salary range for the role early in the process, as it may be below the expectations of someone from the commercial business world.

Thanks,

Khalil Malouf  
BFSI, Executive Director

---

**To:** Khalil Malouf  
**From:** Celine Pittman  
**Date:** October 21  
**Subject:** Re: Candidate for assistant director opening

Hello Mr. Malouf,

After receiving your e-mail, I researched Mr. Reese online, and I agree with your assessment. However, let me direct your attention to Section 2 of the ethics code that we added to the employee handbook last year. It reads:

- Executives may not work for a supplier to the BFSI in any capacity, including as a consultant or a member of its board of directors, without the prior approval of the BFSI's board of directors.

Obviously, this complicates the matter. Would you still like me to move forward with your request?

Best,

Celine Pittman  
Human Resources Manager

**Voluntary Cancellation and Refund Policy**

Tickets purchased directly from Aloft Airlines can be canceled through our Web site or reservation phone line. To request a cancellation, log in to your Aloft Airlines account online and open "My Trips," or call 1-800-555-0129 and select the appropriate option from the menu. For reservations made through a travel agent, please contact the travel agent.

Cancellations made within 24 hours of the ticket purchase qualify the customer for a refund of all fees except the booking fee. After that point, refund eligibility depends on the type of fare purchased. Check your ticket receipt to see the fare rules. Refunds are credited back to the original form of payment. Please allow up to 7 business days for processing.

Involuntary cancellations (those initiated by Aloft Airlines) are subject to different rules. Please see here for details.

Questions 172-175 refer to the following Web page.

https://www.mooretownmall.com/about

**Mooretown Mall**

Built by local developer Leonard Moore over 40 years ago, Mooretown Mall is the oldest continuously operating shopping center in Shannon County. — [1] —. It is owned and managed with care by Mr. Moore's descendants through Moore Enterprises. Spearsley's top shopping and dining destination in its early decades, the mall overcame the closure of many of its tenants during the rise of online shopping. — [2] —. Now occupied by a vibrant variety of businesses, Mooretown Mall often attracts shoppers, diners, and visitors from as far away as Wenglade and Bryantburg.

On its east side, Mooretown Mall is anchored by the largest Clurett Department Store in Greater Ingramville, situated in a striking section that retains the building's original post-modern architecture. Within the mall's west side lie Well Foods supermarket and a five-screen King Cinema, while smaller boutique shops in between specialize in everything from jewelry to video games. Restaurants include Flores Mexican Grill, Harper Steakhouse, and Elena's, and patrons seeking lighter fare can stop at Jaybird Juice or Alden Pretzels. — [3] —. Beyond retail and dining options, Mooretown Mall businesses offer services ranging from yoga classes to eye exams to auto maintenance.

With wheelchair-accessible design, Mooretown Mall is welcoming to people of all physical abilities. — [4] —. Its elaborate seasonal decorations also make it a must-visit destination on major holidays. Proud of its history as a gathering place for several generations of Shannon County residents, Mooretown Mall continues to strive to provide a premier shopping experience.

Questions 176-180 refer to the following work order and e-mail.

**INFORMATION TECHNOLOGY WORK ORDER REQUEST**
**No.:** 8322
**Submitter:** Edward Freeman
**Date Submitted:** August 30, 1:15 P.M.
**Work Location:** Workstation TD030
**Summary:** Data discovery software not needed
**Urgency:** Low ✓ Medium \_**\_ High \_\_**

**Details:**
My work in the textbook development department involves writing content that features imagined characters. The new data discovery program keeps mistaking these characters' names and other information for "sensitive data" and requires me to explain why my writing files don't need security protections. I don't handle any real people's personal information, so the program is unnecessary for me. I would like IT to uninstall it from my computer or at least lower the scanning frequency.

**Technician Assigned:** Jazmin Ortega
**Date Due:** August 31

---

> **To:** Edward Freeman
> **From:** Jazmin Ortega
> **Subject:** IT Work Order Request 8322
> **Date:** August 31

Dear Mr. Freeman,

I have been assigned to assist you with the work order you submitted yesterday regarding Decipher, our data discovery software. I understand your frustration with the program, but the recent tightening of laws around data protection has made this area a priority for the company. Employees are not allowed to remove Decipher completely or reduce the frequency of the scans. However, I can show you how to exempt a folder from scanning. If you save your writing files to that folder, you should no longer be inconvenienced by Decipher.

I want to emphasize to you that this approach puts you at risk of violating our policy regarding the management of real people's sensitive data should you come into possession of some in the future. This would have serious consequences for your employment here. To reduce this risk, you should not extend exempt status to other folders or use your exempt folder to store all your files. If you can agree to these terms, I will be happy to visit your workstation at your convenience.

Jazmin Ortega
Information Technology Specialist

Questions 181-185 refer to the following brochure and e-mail.

Delarosa Catering is the South Coast's premier full-service caterer, handling functions ranging from weddings to corporate trainings. Our chefs design unique dishes that delight clients and showcase the produce of our region's farms. We also strive to help the environment by minimizing our creation of surplus food. For details on how we pursue this goal, visit delarosa.com.

**Our catering process consists of the following four stages:**

- **Stage 1** – Our staff hold an in-person or phone consultation to check your event date, size, and location (events held in one of our affiliated venues are eligible for a discount) and discuss your menu preferences.
- **Stage 2** – A menu is designed and presented to you for feedback. We go through multiple rounds of revision, if necessary, until you finalize it. This stage may also include a tasting session.
- **Stage 3** – We draw up a catering contract specifying the food and serving staff to be provided, prices, and other terms. If it is to your satisfaction, you sign it and pay a deposit.
- **Stage 4** – We make all necessary preparations and cater your event per our agreement.

Interested in hiring Delarosa Catering for your next event? Call us at 555-0174. -------

Questions 186-190 refer to the following chart, letter, and page from a catalog.

**The Farmhouse Cast Iron Process**
_Combining tradition with cutting-edge technology_

1. We melt a special mix of iron and other ingredients.
2. We cast the cookware in traditional sand molds.
3. We put the cooled pieces in a machine that uses tiny steel balls to clean off the sand.
4. We use hand tools to carefully remove any imperfections from the cookware's surface.
5. Depending on the product, we may also apply beautiful enamel or season the cookware with all-natural oils. ("Seasoning", which involves applying and heating an oil coating, protects cast iron from rusting.)

---

Farmhouse Cookware
180 Cline Drive
Allentown, PA 18104

November 8

Amaya Washington
965 Knox Street
Decatur, GA 30030

I recently inherited a Farmhouse cast-iron griddle from my grandmother. It is so easy to cook with, even after being used for decades! I am interested in buying more Farmhouse cookware for my home. I would love to have a 12-inch cast-iron pan—preferably one that is already seasoned—and may purchase a Dutch oven for stews as well. Please send a catalog of your products if there is one available. Thank you.

Sincerely,

Amaya Washington

**Comedy Shows during October**

An hour of stand-up comedy performed by Amy Combs, co-star of the television program _City Living_. Part of a 24-city tour. Topics include exercise and family visits.

**Improv Madness** (Every Tuesday throughout the year, $22)
An hour-long improvisational comedy show by The Improvinators. Completely different every time! Audience participation is welcome.

Questions 196-200 refer to the following instructions, e-mail, and sign.

**Instructions for Accessing Special Collections**

The Vanell University Library's special collections may be viewed only in the Special Collections Reading Room. Those interested in accessing materials in a special collection must follow these steps.

1. Create an account on the special collections Web site.
2. Use the site's search or browsing functions to find materials you wish to view. Check the box next to each and select "Request."
3. Schedule your viewing appointment on the calendar page. To allow time for staff to retrieve your desired materials, you will only be able to select time slots at least two business days in the future.
4. Submit your request. Submitted requests will be viewable and editable on your account page. Before attending your appointment, confirm that your request has been fulfilled, as requests for restricted materials may take longer to process.
5. Review the reading room guidelines to prepare for your visit and learn what items are prohibited.

---

> **From:** Ellen Nganga <ellen.nganga@vanell.edu>
> **To:** Special Collections Desk <specialcollections@vanell.edu>
> **Subject:** Viewing request
> **Date:** October 10

To Whom It May Concern:

I seem to be encountering a problem with the special collections' access request system. I submitted a request on October 1, and its status has been stuck on "Processing" for over a week now. I have missed both the initial appointment I set for October 4 and a second one I then made for October 9.

The previous times I used your Web site to ask to view materials, my requests were fulfilled promptly. It is unclear to me why this time is different. The request is only for some documents related to Kayburg's first mayor, Silas Tanner, which will serve as sources for a paper I am writing for my nineteenth-century politics class.

Could someone look into this matter for me? My need for the documents is time-sensitive, so I hope that the issue can be resolved quickly.

Sincerely,

Ellen Nganga

---

**Special Collections Storage Room 1**

Aisle 1 – Vanell University archives
Aisle 2 – Rare books\*
Aisle 3 – Kayburg history
Aisle 4 – Nora Hogan collection

\*The rare books collection may be handled only by the rare books curator. Staff must direct relevant retrieval requests to them.

### Questions 155–157 refer to the following article.

GOODWIN (June 7)—The Goodwin Arts Council has announced the details of its plans to commemorate the 100th anniversary of Emory Shaffer's birth. The "Emory Shaffer Festival" will take place in the Curry Cultural Center on Saturday, July 24. The bestselling author of such classic children's books as _Adeline and Jeffrey_ and _The Sugar Garden_ was born in Goodwin, though he resided elsewhere as an adult.

The festival will include lively readings of Mr. Shaffer's works and free screenings of their film adaptations, as well as child-friendly craft activities based on the happenings and characters in his stories. In the evening, local actors will stage scenes from the books in the center's auditorium.

Goodwin Arts Council president Soo-Ae Gwon said the festival's organizers hope it captures the author's spirit. "Mr. Shaffer loved to have fun with language, using unusual words and even inventing his own, and that sense of whimsy is what we want to convey to attendees," she said. She promised that the festival would both provide a place for longtime fans to celebrate Mr. Shaffer and encourage young generations' interest in his time-honored stories.

The Goodwin Arts Council is a nonprofit organization that also serves as the local arts agency for the City of Goodwin.

     (C) His enthusiasm for a variety of arts

### About Crawford Solutions

We are a locally-owned business dedicated to designing, printing, and applying eye-catching vinyl wraps and decals for vehicles. A wrap or decal featuring a business's name and logo effectively creates a mobile billboard out of any vehicle, from a compact car to a moving van. We use high-quality cast vinyl and do the printing in-house on a sophisticated large-format inkjet printer.

The heart of Crawford Solutions is our staff, who handle every step of the process with passion and expertise. Our design team will find the best way to showcase your existing branding elements—or make striking graphics from scratch. For example, we recently came up with distinctive construction imagery for full-vehicle wraps for Kline Builders. The company has seen a significant increase in inquiries from potential customers who have seen its colorful trucks on the road and at job sites. Our application team is also top-notch, attaching wraps and decals with both great accuracy and a turnaround time of just one day in most cases.

For a more detailed explanation of what you can expect from our process, as well as examples of our work with past clients to inspire you, please check out the video on this page.

Dear Hiring Manager,

When I saw the opening for a teller position in Roberson Bank's Yates branch, I was eager to apply. My former coworker Adele Hartman, who now works at your Middleton location, speaks very highly of your company, and I am positive that my skills make me an excellent match for the position.

As you can see from my attached résumé, I have been working as a cashier at Kelley Supermarket for the past 18 months. This means I have substantial experience assisting members of the public with financial transactions. In fact, my combination of speed and accuracy in this area even led me to receive the store's Employee of the Month award in December. I have also become adept at building relationships with customers and promoting company products and services based on their needs. As the child of Chinese immigrant parents, I even have verbal fluency in Mandarin, and it would be gratifying to put this asset to use to serve Yates's large population of people of Chinese heritage.

Because I recently began a part-time program to earn my bachelor's degree, I now need a position with fixed hours, which the supermarket cannot provide. The conditions of bank teller work would be a perfect fit with the evening classes offered by my program. I am very interested in this position and hope to have the chance to discuss it with you further.

Sincerely,  
Jacob Wang

### Questions 172–175 refer to the following article.

**Maddox Event Includes Announcement of Timely 3**

SAN JOSE (November 14)—One of the product releases announced by tech giant Maddox at its annual "Next at Maddox" press event today was the next version of its popular smart watch. The Timely 3, which will feature a three-in-one BioTrack health sensor and a Niqua Artisan operating system, will go on sale on December 4.

The Timely 3 will offer a new "Tactile" model with a rotatable bezel (face frame) to control the watch in addition to the standard touch screen and side buttons. — [1] —. The Timely 3 Tactile will have steep price tags of $399 with Bluetooth connectivity or $349 with LTE connectivity. Fortunately, the Timely 3 Standard's Bluetooth and LTE types will cost just $299 and $249, respectively.

Maddox product manager Evelyn Baker said these features were the result of feedback from Timely 2 users. — [2] —.

"The main improvements that customers have requested are more health tracking capabilities and the option to have a bezel controller," said Ms. Baker. "We are excited to be presenting a device that fulfills these criteria and also has increased access to apps thanks to the Niqua operating system. — [3] —."

Ms. Baker also emphasized a convenient similarity between the Timely 2 and the Timely 3.

"We designed the watch band connecting points so that customers who upgrade can put their existing bands on their new device. — [4] —. We wanted to enable those who have invested in custom accessories to continue to use them."

### Crossroads Café

**Menu Item 19: Crossroads Delight Sandwich**

**STEPS**

1. **Prepare bread**  
   Use a sourdough roll. For a medium sandwich, select a 6-inch roll. For a large sandwich, select a 10-inch roll. Slice the bread in half lengthwise.
2. **Add meat**  
   Use sliced roast turkey. Add 3 slices for medium sandwiches and 5 slices for large sandwiches. If the customer requested a deluxe version, add Swiss cheese and bacon.
3. **Toast**  
   Place in a tray on the toaster. Press the button for the appropriate roll size.
4. **Add vegetables**  
   Use 1 handful of lettuce and 2 tomato slices for a medium sandwich and 2 handfuls of lettuce and 3 tomato slices for a large sandwich. For deluxe sandwiches, spread mashed avocado on the top half of bread.
5. **Add sauce**  
   Choose the Delight sauce bottle. Drizzle 2 lines of sauce along the vegetables.
6. **Finish**  
   Cut large sandwiches in half. Place the sandwich on wax paper for serving. For takeout orders, use tape to seal the paper, then write the sandwich's name on top.

---

---

### National Postal Service

**Clarkton Branch**  
**Notice to Employees**

Shifts in the geographical distribution of Clarkton's population have altered the city's postal delivery service needs. Accordingly, we will be adjusting our delivery routes as described below, effective September 1. Mail carriers will receive information about route reassignments soon.

- **Route A** will continue to serve central and northern Clarkton. Its western boundaries will remain Haynes Street and Tate Avenue, but its main eastern boundary will be shifted one block to Park Street.
- **Route B** will now serve all addresses east of Park Street, which will greatly expand its southern area. It will also encompass Galindo Estates.
- **Route C** will begin serving a reduced area of southern Clarkton between Park Street and Delaney Road and no longer include Galindo Estates and Whitaker Office Park. These changes will make its mail volume roughly similar to those of the other routes.
- **Route D** will continue to serve northwestern Clarkton from Haynes Street and Tate Avenue, but also extend southward and eastward all the way to Delaney Road and Whitaker Office Park.

### Questions 186–190 refer to the following flyer, schedule, and e-mail.

---

### Everyday Science Talks 🔬

The Murray Museum of Science & Technology will be holding a series of talks on the theme of science in everyday life. We have invited speakers with expertise in a range of subjects. Have you ever sat in an airplane and wondered how such a heavy machine is able to cruise through the clouds? Sophia Reilly, the museum's newest senior program manager, will give a talk on the science of flight. No matter the field, attendees will learn how earlier researchers and theorists made its major advancements and gain an understanding of the ideas underlying those advancements. Many will even leave with some valuable health advice or tips relating to their favorite hobbies.

The talks will take place once or twice a month on Wednesdays, beginning February 26. Admission is free for museum members and $7 for non-members. Visit www.mmst.org/est to register.

---

---

### Everyday Science Series – Schedule

**February 26**  
Taking to the Sky – Learn about the physics principles that allow us to travel by air from a former employee of the National Aerospace Research Agency (NARA).

**March 26**  
Our Invisible Companions – Microscopic germs are everywhere. Discover how they cause disease and how good hygiene can protect our community.

**April 16**  
Body Fuel – Make healthy choices for your body by learning how to apply the science of nutrition to your diet.

**May 7**  
Growing Science – Even experienced gardeners will find useful information in this overview of research on plants, soil, and pests.

**May 28**  
A Taste of Science – Improve your skills in the kitchen with insights from physics, chemistry, and biology.

**June 18**  
Rain or Shine – Attendees will find out how temperature, air pressure, wind, and other factors determine our weather.

---

## READING TEST (Test 7)

In the Reading test, you will read a variety of texts and answer several different types of reading comprehension questions. The entire Reading test will last 75 minutes. There are three parts, and directions are given for each part. You are encouraged to answer as many questions as possible within the time allowed.

You must mark your answers on the separate answer sheet. Do not write your answers in your test book.

>
> From that point, all you will have to do is replace the cartridges each time a new shipment arrives. We even make eco-friendly disposal of empty cartridges easy by providing a pouch you can use to return them to us for recycling.

Questions 176-180 refer to the following e-mail and review.

> E-Mail Message
>
> **To:** Ethan Frazier
> **From:** Phoebe Dodson
> **Subject:** Design concern
> **Date:** January 12
>
> Hi Ethan,
>
> I appreciate your sending over your preliminary design and sketches for Eagle's Adventure's queuing area on schedule. Your layout uses the available space cleverly and the decorations fit the tone I visualized for this ride. However, I'm concerned that video screens playing footage of eagles won't be sufficient to keep our park's guests entertained while they wait in line. Remember, the attraction that used to occupy this space, Ocean Journey, was infamous among guests for having a boring waiting experience, so we need to put some extra effort into this part this time. Please explore some creative solutions to this problem in your next round of drawings.
>
> Let's set the due date for those designs as this Friday. That should still give us plenty of time to finalize them before February 3, when the procurement team needs to begin sourcing materials.
>
> Phoebe Dodson
> Project Manager, Funworld Amusement Park

> ### Review of Funworld's Eagle's Adventure
>
> By Hwa-Young Im, May 25
>
> The newest addition to Funworld's Animal Kingdom section is a thrilling experience for children and adults alike. Though motion simulator rides are unpopular with some guests, Eagle's Adventure thankfully avoids the common pitfall of the type. No one in my party suffered the motion sickness that such rides often cause. Instead, everyone enjoyed the chance to experience the world from an eagle's perspective. Seated in a cart shaped like the bird, Eagle's Adventure riders swoop and soar together through forests, past waterfalls, and over other animals. The graphics are beautifully rendered on the enormous screen, and there are exciting water and lighting effects. The attraction also improves upon its predecessor, Ocean Journey, by treating those waiting in line to videos of eagles, photo opportunities with eagle statues, and chances to touch real eagle feathers.
>
> Eagle's Adventure is already open at Funworld Moorland and will launch at the Funworld park in Larkhaven on June 4. Guests must be at least 40 inches tall to board the ride.

Questions 181-185 refer to the following article and survey.

> Elvet Now Home to Oswald Cinema
>
> DURHAM (4 May)—Those driving along Hollingside Lane may be surprised to see a replica of the city's long-gone Oswald Cinema standing on the other side of the hedge. The stunning brick building is the centrepiece of Elvet Museum's new 1950s-themed section. Museum director Ah-Yoon Ryu hosted local officials yesterday for the section's ribbon-cutting ceremony.
>
> The sprawling "living museum" complex now features recreations of four periods in the history of Northern England, starting with the 1820s. Each section is staffed with a cast of actors trained to play roles ranging from miners to nurses.
>
> The new Oswald is a functional cinema, with more than 200 seats. It screens classics by Preston Gilbert and other big names of 1950s cinema, as well as period newsreels and adverts. Souvenir tickets and tours of the projection booth are also available.
>
> Other highlights of Elvet's newest section include recreations of an electrical shop, a typical family home, and a community centre. > Hello All,

> The next meeting of Trinity Hotels' Corporate Sustainability Committee will take place this Thursday at 2 P.M. via teleconference.
>
> I will be unable to attend, but Wayne Sweeney will act as chair instead and oversee the agenda. He will also send out the meeting room link.
>
> Asako Miyake is scheduled to present initial feedback from guests on the recent change to our chain's toiletries.
>
> The committee should then choose a supplier for the electric vehicle (EV) charging stations to be installed in each location's covered parking area. Pavel Babkin has obtained pricing and timeline estimates from several companies.

> ⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐

> This past weekend I visited the Kirby Airport location of Trinity Hotel for the second time. My room was just as clean and comfortable as before, and I was pleased to see that the hotel had made some environmentally-friendly upgrades. The small disposable bottles of shampoo and conditioner had been replaced with large, refillable dispensers. Also, my rental car this time was electric, so I got to try the fancy charging stations with the Trinity Hotel logo in the car park. They were very fast! I heartily recommend Trinity Hotel Kirby Airport to other travelers.
>
> — Rebecca Pierce

> ### Customer Experience Survey Results Report
>
> **Client:** Iris Garden Center
> **Report by:** Chan-Woo Kim, Acumen Consumer Research
>
> **Average Ratings (1 = Very poor, 5 = Very good)**
>
> |                    |      |
> | :----------------- | :--- |
> | Store layout       | 2.34 |
> | Product selection  | 3.61 |
> | Product pricing    | 4.83 |
> | Customer service   | 4.02 |
> | Overall experience | 3.56 |
>
> **Discussion:** The average ratings displayed above were generated from 72 submitted surveys. The respondents' ratings and accompanying comments suggest some steps Iris Garden Center could take to improve customers' experiences. First, customers reported some trouble locating products in the center's outdoor spaces, mainly because of a lack of signage. Acumen recommends adding standing signs where possible and floor plans at the entrances. Customers were pleased with the staff's helpfulness but would prefer not to rely on them for finding their way.
>
> Another type of feedback found in several responses related to product selection. Customers are satisfied with your inventory of basic items—plants and gardening equipment. However, they would like to see some garden ornaments, too; suggestions included specially-shaped planters, sculptures, and bird baths.

Questions 196-200 refer to the following review and e-mails.

> ### Hobbs Tries Her Hand at Film
>
> By Trent Atkinson
>
> BLACKWELL (September 5)—Diana Hobbs' first film, _The New Sound_, premiered at the Blackwell International Film Festival today. The accomplished singer-songwriter has made a highly watchable documentary on the so-called "Owensby sound," a genre of country music most popular in the 1960s.
>
> The Owensby sound was characterized by fast tempos and electric instruments instead of the slower, orchestral tunes that were being produced in Hardin, the center of country music at the time. Ms. Hobbs' film traces the style's development and popularization by Owensby-based performers like Sadie Lowery and Boone Sheppard. She includes album clips and some archival video footage of their entertaining live performances, which often featured outlandish costumes. The highlight of the film, however, is her interviews with Mr. Sheppard, whom she knows from her childhood in Galstead, where Mr. Sheppard retired. They have a charming rapport, and he has unique insight into the genre.
>
> The film moves quickly and offers plenty of amusement for country music fans and newcomers alike. Ms. Hobbs has made a promising debut.

> _E-Mail_
>
> **From:** Trent Atkinson
> **To:** Rina Kaneko
> **Subject:** RE: Invitation
> **Date:** September 12
>
> Dear Ms. Kaneko,
>
> I will indeed be flying to the Full Volume Film Festival next month in my capacity as the _Bradford Herald_'s film critic. Thank you for your invitation to lead the question-and-answer session with Ms. Hobbs after her film's screening there. I welcome the opportunity to ask her my own questions and facilitate the discussion with her hometown crowd. I am sure that the event will be quite lively!
>
> Will Mr. Sheppard be in attendance as well? If so, I will prepare some questions specifically for him.
>
> Sincerely,
>
> Trent Atkinson
> Film Critic
> Bradford Herald

> _E-Mail_
>
> **From:** Rina Kaneko
> **To:** Trent Atkinson
> **Subject:** RE: RE: Invitation
> **Date:** September 13
>
> Dear Mr. Atkinson,
>
> Ms. Hobbs and I are delighted to hear that you can moderate the Q&A session. She has been browsing all your pieces for the _Herald_ since I showed her the one on _The New Sound_, and she believes your sensibilities are very similar to hers.
>
> As of now, Mr. Sheppard is not expected to join the session due to health reasons. However, the festival organizers will let you know if this changes, as well as provide details such as where and when you should arrive. I will inform them of your participation today.
>
> Sincerely,
>
> Rina Kaneko

As the new owner of a Duramark printer, we want you to know that our commitment
to customers does not end at producing high-quality electronics. We also offer Quick
Ink, a convenient way to ensure your printer is always fully stocked with ink by having
cartridges shipped to your door automatically.
Competing subscription services will send you ink at fixed intervals whether you need
more or not. With Quick Ink, special ink cartridges allow your printer to monitor its ink
levels. The device will then send an alert to your local Duramark distribution center
shortly before replacement cartridges become necessary. The process is simple and
sustainable.
Pricing for Quick Ink is based on the number of pages you print per month, not the
number of ink cartridges. Every page costs the same, even high-resolution photos.
There are a variety of subscription plans for homes and small businesses, starting from
$1.50 per month for up to 10 pages.
To get started with Quick Ink, visit www.quickink.duramark.com and click "Enroll." You
will be asked to create a Duramark account if you do not already have one. Once you
have chosen and paid for your plan, you can expect your first shipment within seven
business days.
From that point, all you will have to do is replace the cartridges each time a new
shipment arrives. We even make eco-friendly disposal of empty cartridges easy by

E-Mail Message
To: Ethan Frazier
From: Phoebe Dodson
Subject: Design concern
Date: January 12
Hi Ethan,
I appreciate your sending over your preliminary design and sketches for Eagle's
Adventure's queuing area on schedule. Your layout uses the available space cleverly and
the decorations fit the tone I visualized for this ride. However, I'm concerned that video
screens playing footage of eagles won't be sufficient to keep our park's guests entertained
while they wait in line. Remember, the attraction that used to occupy this space, Ocean
Journey, was infamous among guests for having a boring waiting experience, so we need
to put some extra effort into this part this time. Please explore some creative solutions to
this problem in your next round of drawings.
Let's set the due date for those designs as this Friday. That should still give us plenty of
time to finalize them before February 3, when the procurement team needs to begin
sourcing materials.
Phoebe Dodson
Project Manager, Funworld Amusement Park
Review of Funworld's Eagle's Adventure
By Hwa-Young Im, May 25
The newest addition to Funworld's Animal Kingdom section is a thrilling experience for
children and adults alike. Though motion simulator rides are unpopular with some guests,
Eagle's Adventure thankfully avoids the common pitfall of the type. No one in my party
suffered the motion sickness that such rides often cause. Instead, everyone enjoyed the
chance to experience the world from an eagle's perspective. Seated in a cart shaped like
the bird, Eagle's Adventure riders swoop and soar together through forests, past waterfalls,
and over other animals. The graphics are beautifully rendered on the enormous screen,
and there are exciting water and lighting effects. The attraction also improves upon its
predecessor, Ocean Journey, by treating those waiting in line to videos of eagles, photo
opportunities with eagle statues, and chances to touch real eagle feathers.
Eagle's Adventure is already open at Funworld Moorland and will launch at the Funworld
park in Larkhaven on June 4. Guests must be at least 40 inches tall to board the ride.

Questions 181-185 refer to the following article and survey.
Elvet Now Home to Oswald Cinema
DURHAM (4 May)-Those driving along
Hollingside Lane may be surprised to see
a replica of the city's long-gone Oswald
Cinema standing on the other side of the
hedge. The stunning brick building is
the centrepiece of Elvet Museum's new
1950s-themed section. Museum director
Ah-Yoon Ryu hosted local officials
yesterday for the section's ribbon-cutting
ceremony.
The sprawling "living museum"
complex now features recreations of four
periods in the history of Northern England,
starting with the 1820s. Each section is
staffed with a cast of actors trained to play
roles ranging from miners to nurses.
The new Oswald is a functional cinema,
with more than 200 seats. It screens classics
by Preston Gilbert and other big names of
1950s cinema, as well as period newsreels
and adverts. Souvenir tickets and tours of
the projection booth are also available.
Other highlights of Elvet's newest
section include recreations of an electrical
shop, a typical family home, and a
community centre. Visitors can even refresh
themselves at a café selling real traditional
treats and playing popular music from the
1950s. For passes or more information, visit
www.elvetmuseum.org.uk.

Thank you for visiting Elvet Museum. We would appreciate your feedback on your

Questions 196-200 refer to the following review and e-mails.
222
會 Hobbs Tries Her Hand at Film
By Trent Atkinson
BLACKWELL (September 5)-Diana Hobbs' first film, The New Sound, premiered at
the Blackwell International Film Festival today. The accomplished singer-songwriter
has made a highly watchable documentary on the so-called "Owensby sound," a
genre of country music most popular in the 1960s.
The Owensby sound was characterized by fast tempos and electric instruments
instead of the slower, orchestral tunes that were being produced in Hardin, the
center of country music at the time. Ms. Hobbs' film traces the style's development
and popularization by Owensby-based performers like Sadie Lowery and Boone
Sheppard. She includes album clips and some archival video footage of their
entertaining live performances, which often featured outlandish costumes. The
highlight of the film, however, is her interviews with Mr. Sheppard, whom she knows
from her childhood in Galstead, where Mr. Sheppard retired. They have a charming
rapport, and he has unique insight into the genre. The film moves quickly and offers plenty of amusement for country music fans and
newcomers alike. Ms. Hobbs has made a promising debut.
*E-Mail*

| **Date:**    | September 1                              |
| **Subject:** | Fall Fair                                |

Dear Third Street Businesses:

This message is to inform you about road closures necessitated by the Winslow Fall Fair. From 8 A.M. to 9 P.M. on Saturday, October 2, there will be no vehicle access to Third Street between Armstrong Road and Clarke Drive. However, you may rest assured that all sidewalks will remain open.

The opening hours of the Fall Fair are from 10 A.M. to 7 P.M. As in previous years, there will be around 60 vendor booths and food trucks, plus music playing through loudspeakers. We are also running a trial of including carnival rides. Approximately 5,000 attendees are expected. If you have inquiries about this event, please contact our office at 555-0184.

Maitreyi Singh, Director

Calvin Hurst  
3117 Cardenas Drive  
Hoover, AL 35216

Dear Mr. Hurst,

As the year draws to a close, I wanted to write to thank you for your efforts as an expert volunteer here at the Birmingham Literacy Center. Your assistance in streamlining our organization's accounting system and preparing financial statements has been crucial to our growth. In fact, we recently learned that we have been awarded a sizeable grant from the Jennings Foundation, and its selection committee specifically cited our financial processes and reporting as one of the deciding factors.

#### Renata Schaefer

Questions 161-163 refer to the following article.

### Treichl Changes Direction on Feature

**FRANKFURT (18 September)**—Treichl, Inc., one of the world's premier vehicle manufacturers, will no longer oblige customers to join a paid subscription service to make use of their car's seat-heating hardware. The company had introduced the practice last year, drawing widespread criticism from automotive enthusiasts. Its newest vehicles, announced this week, either come with fully functional heated seats or do not have them at all.

"Our intention behind the offering was to give customers flexibility," Treichl's vice president of sales, Beatrix Van Eyck, said when reached for comment. "But people perceived it as having to pay double for a feature, and subscriber numbers were low. We have no plans to maintain or expand subscriptions for factory-installed hardware at present."

However, Ms. Van Eyck noted that the company continues to charge customers monthly for some software features available in their cars, such as parking assistance and DriveLink, its digital service platform with capabilities such as weather and traffic updates.

"Customers do seem to appreciate the ability to upgrade, downgrade, or suspend these functions whenever they want, especially when improved versions become available," said Ms. Van Eyck.

161. What was special about the vehicles that Treichl released last year?
     (A) A change to the usual design of the seats
     (B) The requirement to pay continuously for an amenity
     (C) The ability to choose whether some hardware is included
     (D) A revision to the warranty policy for a heating function

     (A) To promote a service offering
     (B) To issue an apology to customers
     (C) To discuss a new vehicle
     (D) To justify a company decision

163. What does the article suggest about Treichl, Inc.?

     (B) It experienced a drop in vehicle sales last year.
     (C) Its factories had difficulty installing some hardware.
     (D) It is about to release a new version of some automotive software.

Neil Olsen

Questions 181-185 refer to the following review and e-mail.

> ### Fashion Unearthed
>
> **Reviewed by Evelyn Dudley**
>
> Seiji Otori loves fashion, but he also loves our planet. In his new book, _Fashion Unearthed: An Environmentalist's Guide to Looking Good_, he explores ways that shopping enthusiasts can indulge their passion for style with minimal harm to the environment. The book includes an impressive catalogue of constructive suggestions, all supported by in-depth research. Overall, Mr. Otori has created a good introduction to issues in the fashion world that focuses on practical options available to consumers.
>
> Otori's book is impressive in its scope, as it touches on everything from textile production to retail distribution and recycling practices. He even discusses online reselling platforms, a newer phenomenon that is important because of its popularity and mixed environmental effects. In fact, the only conspicuous omission is that of clothing repair and alteration services, which should be recommended as ways to refresh worn or outdated pieces. This oversight aside, _Fashion Unearthed_ is an informative read with an engagingly optimistic outlook.

---

| Field        | Details                  |
| :----------- | :----------------------- |
| **From:**    | s.otori@mailit.com       |
| **To:**      | editor@currentliving.com |
| **Subject:** | Fashion Unearthed review |
| **Date:**    | May 29                   |

Dear Editor:

It was a thrill to see my book reviewed in your magazine by Evelyn Dudley. I consider her one of the inspirations for _Fashion Unearthed_, as it was reading her works in college that first got me interested in the issues I discuss in the book. I was pleased that she highlighted the chapter on reselling apps and Web sites, since I also feel that area has not been deeply examined in other publications. She also makes a reasonable point that the range of recommendations could have been wider. In fact, my first draft of the book did include a section on the missing topic she mentioned, but it was cut to streamline the book. Perhaps it could be restored in a future edition.

Seiji Otori

### New Pastry Manager at Brinkley

**PIERPONT (January 4)**—Brinkley Culinary Academy announced yesterday that Grady Freeman has been appointed as the manager of its pastry program. Mr. Freeman joins Brinkley from the high-end Abbottville restaurant L'Artisan, where he was the head pastry chef.

Mr. Freeman discovered a passion for making sweet treats by chance at his first job at the Lynn's Bakery chain. He then earned a pastry certification from the Daucourt School of Culinary Arts. His résumé includes positions at illustrious establishments in France and Switzerland, including the five-star Millésime.

While at L'Artisan, Mr. Freeman also competed successfully in national pastry-making contests and taught short workshops at Brinkley and other culinary institutions.

---

> ### Brinkley Culinary Academy
>
> **Renowned Chefs Series for Fall**
>
> Workshops exclusively for Brinkley students, led by:
>
> - Celia Fischer of Stockholm Bakery—Swedish pastries and desserts (September 12)
> - Eun-Sol Min of Ganjang—The art of Korean fermentation (October 10)
> - Stepan Mukhin of The Volga—Trends in Russian cuisine (November 14)
> - Adrien Borges of La Colombe—French seafood dishes (December 12)
>
> Each workshop will conclude with a tasting. Admission is free but limited to 30 people. Register in advance by visiting the Administration Office (Roth Hall, Room 101).

---

| **To:**      | Grady Freeman <gfreeman@bca.org> |
| **Subject:** | Greetings                        |
| **Date:**    | September 2                      |

Hi Grady,

How have you been doing since you left L'Artisan? Are you enjoying working at Brinkley? I'm asking both because I'd like to catch up with you and because I'm actually considering making a similar transition. The late hours and stressful pace of restaurant work are becoming tiring. I'd be very interested to hear how you're finding academic life and whether you think it might suit me.

In fact, if you have time, I'd like to discuss this in person. I can come to you. One of Brinkley's Renowned Chefs this fall, Eun-Sol Min, is an old coworker and friend of mine. I haven't seen her since she left Abbottville for Seoul several years ago, so we made plans to spend time together while she's in the area for her workshop. Perhaps you and I can meet up on campus around that time, too. Let me know.

Best,  
Amber
